  • fbsN, sbsN — First and second interaction points, compact set; [n_rayN, 3]
  • no_interact — Mesh-hit count per ray, full ray set; [n_ray]
  • fbs_angleN — Incidence angle at FBS (the angle to the face, as ray_mesh_interact writes it), compact set; [n_rayN]
  • normal_vecN — FBS and SBS normals [Nx_F Ny_F Nz_F Nx_S Ny_S Nz_S], compact set; [n_rayN, 6]; required. The plane of the VBS, and the test whether the two faces of an entry are parallel.
  • out_typeN — Interaction type code from ray_mesh_interact, compact set; [n_rayN]. Its total-reflection bit is re-evaluated at the VBS angle; a thin-sheet code takes the sheet path.
  • mtl_prop — Material properties keyed by column name (the csv_prop output of obj_file_read); at most 16383 materials.
  • mtl_ind_fbsN, mtl_ind_sbsN — Material indices of the FBS / SBS faces, compact set; [n_rayN] (0 = air). The sheet case of a hit is re-derived from the code and the two faces' materials.
  • mtl_ind_prev_in, mtl_ind_current_in, mtl_ind_buffer_in — State words, full ray set; [n_ray], read at g, never written. NULL reads as state 0 (in air, no flags). Pass the words an earlier call wrote through unchanged.

    Word Bits 0-13 Bit 14 Bit 15
    mtl_ind_current medium the ray is in, 0 = air bent: the leg follows a bend traced with type 2 or 5; zero in air the leg's series was summed
    mtl_ind_prev medium the leg was entered from, or the sheet crossed or reflected off a sheet in this word lies inside the current medium; with a solid, the face into the current medium was evaluated at a real-invariant cosine the leg's faces are not parallel
    mtl_ind_buffer deferred medium followed, with a zero index: the leg began at a reflection that left the series to the tracer. With an index: the layer of an ordered stack behind the ray that the previous-medium word does not name clear
    mtl_ind_buffer thickness code, bits 0-14 (part of the code) set: mass-crossed
    Under bit 15 the buffer word is the normal thickness, as a logarithmic code, of a mass-law leg closed on the way into the current one (bonded mass-law bodies are one plate); it is exempt from the material range check.
  • path_dir_prev — Physical ray direction entering this segment, full ray set; [n_ray, 3]
  • acc_dist_geometric — Accumulated geometric in-layer distance carried into this call, full ray set; [n_ray]. NULL reads as 0. Frequency-independent, so a multi-frequency caller keeps one instance.
  • acc_dist_refracted — Accumulated refracted (VBS) in-layer distance carried into this call, full ray set; [n_ray]. NULL reads as 0. Frequency-dependent (the bend follows the material at center_frequency), so a multi-frequency caller keeps one instance per frequency.
  • ray_indN — Compact-to-full ray index map; [n_rayN] to [n_ray]; NULL = identity (n_ray == n_rayN)
  • eps (optional) — Threshold on the round-trip amplitude ρ (ρ ≥ 0, not bounded by one: next to a lossy or evanescent layer a face can reflect more than one in amplitude): a series is summed where ρ ≥ eps and left to the tracer where ρ &lt; eps. eps = 0 always sums (for callers that cannot re-emit); eps ≥ 1 leaves every series with ρ &lt; eps to the tracer. A layer whose faces are known not to be parallel is left to the tracer for any eps > 0. A thin sheet's own series is always summed (there is no interior to trace); eps gates the host layer a sheet lies in. Default 0.15.
  • verdict_inN (optional) — The verdict of each event as an earlier call on the same events wrote it to verdict_outN, compact set; [n_rayN]. Where given, a series is summed exactly where the entry is nonzero and eps is not read: a multi-frequency caller hands the base frequency's verdicts to the other frequencies, whose traced paths are the base frequency's. NULL: this call decides by eps.
  • tridir (optional) — Vertex-ray directions of the beam entering this segment, Cartesian, as handed to ray_mesh_interact; [n_ray, 9]. Given together with tridirN; requires origN and destN
Outputs:
  • mtl_ind_prev_outN, mtl_ind_current_outN, mtl_ind_buffer_outN — Updated state words (layout above), compact set; [n_rayN]. The previous-medium word may hold a sheet: the boundary behind the ray, never a medium it travels in. NULL skips the write.
  • gainN (in/out) — Per-interaction linear power gain, updated in place; [n_rayN]. Kept consistent with xprmatN. A killed ray (resolved_typeN == 0) has gainN = 0 and an all-zero xprmatN.
  • xprmatN (in/out) — Polarization transfer matrix, updated in place; [8, n_rayN] for EM mode, [2, n_rayN] for scalar mode, layout and basis as in ray_mesh_interact. On return it also carries the in-medium attenuation and excess phase of the leg the ray leaves and, where the series was summed, the factor S. Spreading loss stays excluded, so the matrix is the complete per-event Jones factor. Where the series is left to the tracer the matrix carries the first pass only, and the bounces arrive as further events. At a thin-sheet crossing the matrix is replaced by the composite for the media the state reports.
  • path_dirN (in/out) — Continuation direction, corrected in place by the VBS construction, compact set; [n_rayN, 3]
  • origN, destN (optional, in/out) — The relaunch segment ray_mesh_interact built, compact set; [n_rayN, 3], given together. With a refracting type (2, 5) that segment was bent at a real angle in the medium the ray leaves. Where this function writes the direction of a wave out of a lossy medium whose in-medium cosine it recovered (a bare exit, a glued exit, a transition into another solid), both points are put on one line along path_dirN, through the SBS of a co-located pair and the FBS otherwise. Every other ray, and every other type, is left untouched. A multi-frequency caller passes them on the call that defines the geometry only.
  • tridirN (optional, in/out) — Vertex-ray directions leaving, Cartesian, compact set; [n_rayN, 9]. Corrected together with origN and destN: each vertex ray gets the construction of the spine from its own direction in tridir. If one of the three cannot be formed, the ray keeps the geometry it had.
  • verdict_outN (optional) — Per event, 1 where this call summed a bounce series and 0 where it left the bounces to the tracer, compact set; [n_rayN]. NULL skips the write.
  • acc_dist_geometricN — Accumulated geometric distance leaving this call, compact set; [n_rayN]. NULL skips the write. Where the ray goes on inside a medium it includes the relaunch offset of ray_mesh_interact (origN lies that far past the point the ray left at), so the next leg, measured from origN, adds up to the leg from the hit point.
  • acc_dist_refractedN — Accumulated refracted (VBS) distance leaving this call, compact set; [n_rayN]. NULL skips the write. It includes the relaunch offset the same way, projected onto the direction the wave has (exact for parallel faces).
  • resolved_typeN (optional) — Resolved interaction-type code, bit-encoded (qd::bits<uint8_t>), compact set; [n_rayN]. 0 = ray killed. NULL skips the write.

    Bit Flag Meaning
    0 ok OK flag (0 = a deferred medium is pending in the buffer word)
    1 vbs VBS correction (gain/xprmat corrected at the VBS instead of FBS/SBS)
    2 resolve Internal multi-bounce series accounted for (expect no further internal bounces)
    3 inside Inside-object flag (1 = ray continues inside, 0 = continues outside)
    4 fix Fix flag (the exit face names another medium than the state, or a sheet event met a deferred medium)
    5 tir Total-reflection flag (also set when a transmission factor forced reflection)
    6 trans/sheet See the qualification below
    7 refl Reflection flag (0 = transmission/refraction, 1 = reflection/scattering)
    Bit 6 is qualified by bit 2 (a transparent interface sums no series, and scattering is not implemented):

    Bit 6 Bit 2 Meaning
    1 0 Transparent interface (transmission) / scatter (reflection)
    1 1 Thin-sheet crossing
    In the thin-sheet region bit 1 carries the glued verdict instead of the VBS flag (a sheet is always re-resolved at the VBS angle):

    Bit 1 (sheet region) Meaning
    0 Isolated — the same medium bounds both sides of the sheet
    1 Glued — the two bounding media differ
    The sheet region reports the resolved state, not the sheet topology. The codes 69 and 71 below are unrelated to the out_typeN codes of the same value. Reachable composite values for transmission / refraction:

    Dec Hex FIX TIR Flags set Meaning
    9 0x09 — 41 inside, ok o-i entry, OR i-i transition (refr. 2/5, FBS==VBS)
    8 0x08 — 40 inside o-i entry, deferred buffer set (overlap/edge)
    11 0x0B 27 43, 59 inside, vbs, ok i-i transition (undev. 1/4, VBS relocated)
    13 0x0D 29 45, 61 inside, resolve, ok i-i transition + slab series (refr. 2/5, FBS==VBS)
    15 0x0F 31 47, 63 inside, resolve, vbs, ok i-i transition + slab series (undev. 1/4, VBS)
    1 0x01 17 33, 49 ok i-o exit (refr. 2/5, FBS==VBS)
    3 0x03 19 35, 51 vbs, ok i-o exit (undev. 1/4, VBS relocated)
    5 0x05 21 37, 53 resolve, ok i-o exit + slab series (refr. 2/5, FBS==VBS)
    7 0x07 23 39, 55 resolve, vbs, ok i-o exit + slab series (undev. 1/4, VBS)
    73 0x49 89 — trans, inside, ok ignore-hit / same-medium pass, no gain change
    72 0x48 88 — trans, inside nested pass-through, buffer deferred
    65 0x41 — — trans, ok advance to ray destination, identity interface
    69 0x45 85 101 sheet, resolve, ok isolated sheet crossed in air
    77 0x4D 93 109 sheet, resolve, inside, ok isolated sheet crossed inside a medium (embedded)
    71 0x47 87 103 sheet, glued, resolve, ok glued exit, solid \ sheet \ air
    79 0x4F 95 111 sheet, glued, resolve, inside, ok glued entry, host \ sheet \ solid
    Reachable composite values for reflection:

    Dec Hex FIX TIR Flags set Meaning
    129 0x81 — 161 refl, ok eager front reflection (R0), outside (FBS==VBS)
    137 0x89 153 169,185 refl, inside, ok internal back-reflection (incoming refr. 2/5, FBS==VBS)
    139 0x8B 155 171,187 refl, inside, vbs, ok internal back-reflection (incoming undev. 1/4, VBS)
    141 0x8D 157 173,189 refl, inside, resolve, ok internal back-reflection + slab series (incoming refr. 2/5)
    143 0x8F 159 175,191 refl, inside, resolve, vbs, ok internal back-reflection + slab series (incoming undev. 1/4)
    197 0xC5 213 229 refl, sheet, resolve, ok reflection off an isolated sheet, ray outside
    205 0xCD 221 237 refl, sheet, resolve, inside, ok reflection off an isolated sheet, ray inside a medium
    199 0xC7 215 231 refl, sheet, glued, resolve, ok reflection off a glued sheet from the host side
    207 0xCF 223 239 refl, sheet, glued, resolve, inside, ok reflection off a glued sheet from the solid side
    192+ 0xC0+ — — refl, scatter, resolve clear reserved: scattering not implemented
    A stack of two different sheets is crossed as one event with the codes of a sheet; a singular stack kills the ray.
See also:
  • The quadriga-lib Material Model and Ray-Surface Physics (companion document)
  • ray_mesh_interact (computes the per-interaction Fresnel/Jones result this function corrects)
  • calc_diffraction_gain (uses this function for its media)
  • ray_progress (the tracer that calls it)

ray_subdivide_flag
Compute the per-ray subdivision decision from the beam-tube footprint at the first bounce
  • Projects the three vertex rays of each beam tube onto the plane of the first-bounce face and measures the longest edge of the resulting wavefront triangle
  • Flags a ray when that edge exceeds subdivision_tolerance_m and the ray is still eligible to be split, i.e. it travels outside a medium and has not reached its subdivision or interaction limit
  • The decision is purely geometric: the vertex origins on the face are the same for reflection, transmission and refraction, so no material data, second-bounce index or frequency is needed
  • This is the single source of truth for the subdivision decision. ray_progress consumes the result when it is passed in and calls this function itself otherwise, so a caller that needs to know the outcome in advance — a shading pass that must not commit beams which will reappear as sub-beams — gets exactly the set that will actually be split
Declaration:
std::vector<bool> quadriga_lib::ray_subdivide_flag(
    const arma::fmat &mesh,
    const arma::fmat &orig,
    const arma::fmat &dest,
    const arma::u32_vec &fbs_ind,
    const arma::fmat &trivec,
    const arma::fmat &tridir,
    const std::vector<quadriga_lib::path> &paths,
    const arma::Col<short> &mtl_ind_current,
    uint8_t max_no_interactions = 20,
    uint8_t max_no_subdivisions = 2,
    float subdivision_tolerance_m = 3.0f);
Inputs:
  • mesh — Faces of the triangular mesh; each row: {x1 y1 z1 x2 y2 z2 x3 y3 z3}; [n_mesh, 9]
  • orig — Ray origins in GCS; [n_ray, 3]. Defines n_ray; must be non-empty
  • dest — Ray destinations in GCS; [n_ray, 3]
  • fbs_ind — 1-based index of the first intersected mesh element, 0 = no hit; [n_ray]. Obtained from ray_triangle_intersect for the same orig / dest pair
  • trivec — Beam wavefront triangle vertices relative to the ray origin; [n_ray, 9]
  • tridir — Vertex-ray directions, Cartesian; [n_ray, 9]. Need not be unit length
  • paths — Per-ray path objects; n_ray entries. Only the subdivision counter nSUB and the segment count are read
  • mtl_ind_current — Current medium state word, 0 = outside; [n_ray]. A ray inside a medium is never split: the sub-beams restart their in-layer accumulator and recompute their direction geometrically, which is only valid outside
  • max_no_interactions (optional) — Total interactions per ray, 0 to 255. A ray that has already reached the limit is not split, so it does not expand into four sub-beams that all terminate in the next generation
  • max_no_subdivisions (optional) — Number of subdivisions per ray, 0 to 255. 0 disables subdivision and the returned flags are all false
  • subdivision_tolerance_m (optional) — Maximum beam-tube edge length before a ray is split, in metres; must be greater than 0
Output:
  • subdiv_flag — true where the ray must be split; n_ray entries. Rays that miss the mesh (fbs_ind = 0) are always false. A beam whose tube only partially covers the face — a vertex ray running parallel to it, pointing away from it, or intersecting absurdly far away — is treated as having an infinite edge and is always flagged
See also:
  • ray_progress (advance one generation of a beam-traced ray set)
  • ray_triangle_intersect (compute fbs_ind)
  • subdivide_rays (split the flagged beams into sub-beams)
  • ray_mesh_interact (reports the same edge length as edge_lengthN)

ray_triangle_intersect
Compute ray-triangle intersections in 3D using the Möller–Trumbore algorithm
  • Counts the total number of intersections between orig and dest
  • Computes the coordinates and object IDs of the first two intersections per ray (FBS/SBS)
  • The AVX2 and CUDA kernels compute in single precision, GENERIC in the precision of the input; with double input, the AVX2 kernel forms the vectors between ray origin, vertices and bounding boxes in double before rounding them, so its precision depends on the size of the scene, not on its distance from the coordinate origin (e.g. UTM coordinates); the CUDA kernel rounds the coordinates themselves, so its errors grow with the distance from the origin
  • Triangle edges and vertices count as hits; hits at the same distance (e.g. on a shared edge) are reported in the order of the mesh index, the lower index first (all kernels)
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::ray_triangle_intersect(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *orig,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *dest,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *mesh,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *fbs = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *sbs = nullptr,
    arma::u32_vec *no_interact = nullptr,
    arma::u32_vec *fbs_ind = nullptr,
    arma::u32_vec *sbs_ind = nullptr,
    const arma::u32_vec *sub_mesh_index = nullptr,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *aabb = nullptr,
    int use_kernel = 0,
    int gpu_id = 0);
Inputs:
  • orig — Ray origins in GCS; [n_ray, 3]
  • dest — Ray destinations in GCS; [n_ray, 3]
  • mesh — Triangular mesh; each row: {x1 y1 z1 x2 y2 z2 x3 y3 z3}; further columns are ignored; [n_mesh, 9]
  • sub_mesh_index (optional) — Start indices of sub-meshes in mesh; enables AABB-accelerated traversal; [n_sub]
  • aabb (optional) — Pre-computed axis-aligned bounding boxes per sub-mesh; each row: {x_min x_max y_min y_max z_min z_max}; if nullptr, AABBs are computed from mesh; [n_sub, 6]
  • use_kernel (optional) — Compute kernel selector: 0 = auto, 1 = GENERIC, 2 = AVX2, 3 = CUDA; throws if unavailable and for other values; auto mode selects CUDA when n_ray >= 500 and CUDA is available, else AVX2, else GENERIC.
  • gpu_id (optional) — CUDA device ID; ignored when not using CUDA
Outputs:
  • fbs (optional) — First-bounce intersection points in GCS; [n_ray, 3]
  • sbs (optional) — Second-bounce intersection points in GCS; [n_ray, 3]
  • no_interact (optional) — Total number of intersections per ray between orig and dest; [n_ray]
  • fbs_ind (optional) — 1-based index of first intersected mesh element; 0 = none; [n_ray]
  • sbs_ind (optional) — 1-based index of second intersected mesh element; 0 = none; [n_ray]
  • fbs and sbs may be the same objects as orig or dest (e.g. to advance rays in place)
See also:
  • obj_file_read (load mesh from OBJ file)
  • triangle_mesh_segmentation (compute sub-mesh indices and AABBs)
  • ray_point_intersect (beam interactions with sampling points)
  • icosphere (generate ray beams)
  • subdivide_rays (split ray beams into sub-beams)

refractive_index
Real refractive index of a homogeneous medium
  • Returns n = Re √(ε_r·μ_r) of material iM, with the ITU-R P.2040 model ε_r = a·(f/fRef)^b − j·17.98·σ/f[GHz], σ = c·(f/fRef)^d, and μ_r from e, f, g, h in the same form. For a material without the mass column the permittivity resonance (resF, resQ, resS) is part of ε_r.
  • For a mass-law material (m > 0) ε_r and μ_r are an impedance mapping and n is not a propagation index.
  • Air (iM = 0) returns 1.
Declaration:
dtype quadriga_lib::refractive_index(
    const std::unordered_map<std::string, std::vector<dtype>> &mtl_prop,
    arma::uword iM,
    dtype center_frequency);
Inputs:
  • mtl_prop — Material properties keyed by column name (the csv_prop output of obj_file_read); each value has length n_mtl
  • iM — 1-based material index (0 = no material / air)
  • center_frequency — Center frequency in [Hz]
Returns:
  • Real refractive index of the medium relative to air
See also:
  • The quadriga-lib Material Model and Ray-Surface Physics (the formulas and the columns)
  • medium_gain (for the distance-dependent in-medium loss)
  • ray_mesh_interact (for complex ray-material interactions)
  • obj_file_read (defines mtl_prop format)

subdivide_rays
Subdivide ray beams into four smaller sub-beams
  • Each triangular beam is split into 4 sub-beams, n_rayN = 4 * n_subdiv rays are written to the output
  • Rays can be selected by index (0-based list), all rays are subdivided if it is not given
  • n_subdiv is the number of selected rays
  • tridir format is auto-detected: spherical [n_ray, 6] or Cartesian [n_ray, 9], output matches the input format
  • Pre-allocated outputs that can hold all new rays are reused as they are and must have the same number of rows, the new rays are written to the first n_rayN rows, leaving the remaining rows untouched. Smaller buffers are re-allocated to that size, discarding their content.
  • Internal math is done in double precision, the new origins stay within 1 ULP of the wavefront plane spanned by orig and trivec, no offset is applied along the propagation direction
  • The direction values of the 3 original vertices are passed through unchanged, only the 3 new edge-midpoint directions are calculated, hence repeated subdivision does not accumulate rounding errors at the corners
  • If transposed_output is true, all outputs are written transposed, i.e. the rays are in the columns and the components in the rows, e.g. origN becomes [3, n_rayN]
Declaration:
arma::uword quadriga_lib::subdivide_rays(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &orig,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &trivec,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &tridir,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *dest = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *origN = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *trivecN = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *tridirN = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *destN = nullptr,
    const arma::u32_vec *index = nullptr);
Inputs:
  • orig — Ray origin points in GCS; [n_ray, 3]
  • trivec — Vectors from origin to the wavefront vertices, columns [x1 y1 z1 x2 y2 z2 x3 y3 z3]; [n_ray, 9]
  • tridir — Vertex-ray directions, spherical [v1az v1el v2az v2el v3az v3el] or Cartesian [v1x v1y v1z v2x v2y v2z v3x v3y v3z]; [n_ray, 6] or [n_ray, 9]
  • dest (optional) — Ray destination points, ignored if empty; [n_ray, 3]
  • index (optional) — 0-based indices of the rays that should be subdivided, may repeat indices and determines the output order; Invalid indices raise an exception after the loop has finished, outputs may be partially written by then; [n_subdiv]
  • transposed_output (optional) — If true, the outputs are written transposed with the rays in the columns, e.g. origN becomes [3, n_rayN]; default = false
Outputs:
  • origN — Subdivided ray origins, centroids of the sub-beam wavefronts; [n_rayN, 3] or [3, n_rayN] for transposed_output
  • trivecN — Subdivided wavefront vectors, relative to origN; [n_rayN, 9] or [9, n_rayN] for transposed_output
  • tridirN — Subdivided vertex-ray directions, same format as tridir; [n_rayN, 6] or [n_rayN, 9] or [6/9, n_rayN] for transposed_output
  • destN — Subdivided destinations, left untouched if dest was nullptr or empty; [n_rayN, 3] or [3, n_rayN] for transposed_output
Returns:
  • n_rayN — Number of rays written to the output, 4 * n_subdiv
See also:
  • icosphere (generate initial beams)
  • ray_point_intersect (beam-sample-point interaction)
  • ray_triangle_intersect (beam-triangle interaction)

subdivide_triangles
Subdivide triangles into smaller triangles
  • Uniformly subdivides each input triangle into n_div x n_div smaller triangles
  • Output count: n_triangles_out = n_triangles_in x n_div x n_div
  • Material indices are duplicated from the parent triangle to all sub-triangles
  • triangles_out may be the same object as triangles_in, and mtl_ind_out the same as mtl_ind (in place)
  • Throws std::invalid_argument when n_div is so large that the number of output values exceeds 64 bits
Declaration:
arma::uword quadriga_lib::subdivide_triangles(
    arma::uword n_div,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *triangles_in,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *triangles_out,
    const arma::uvec *mtl_ind = nullptr,
    arma::uvec *mtl_ind_out = nullptr);
Inputs:
  • n_div — Number of subdivisions per edge
  • triangles_in — Mesh vertices as {x1,y1,z1,x2,y2,z2,x3,y3,z3}; [n_triangles_in, 9]
  • mtl_ind — Material indices per triangle (the csv_ind output of obj_file_read); [n_triangles_in]
Outputs:
  • triangles_out — Subdivided mesh vertices, same column layout as triangles_in; [n_triangles_out, 9]
  • mtl_ind_out — Material indices for subdivided triangles; [n_triangles_out]
Returns:
  • n_triangles_out — Number of generated triangles

triangle_mesh_aabb
Calculate the axis-aligned bounding box (AABB) of a triangle mesh and its sub-meshes
  • Computes the AABB for each sub-mesh; used to accelerate ray tracing by cheaply excluding non-intersecting geometry
  • Each triangle row: {x1, y1, z1, x2, y2, z2, x3, y3, z3}
  • Output columns: {x_min, x_max, y_min, y_max, z_min, z_max}
  • If vec_size > 1, output rows are padded to the next multiple of vec_size
Declaration:
arma::Mat<dtype> quadriga_lib::triangle_mesh_aabb(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *mesh,
    const arma::u32_vec *sub_mesh_index = nullptr,
    arma::uword vec_size = 1);
Inputs:
  • mesh — Triangle mesh vertices in global Cartesian coordinates; further columns are ignored; [n_triangles, 9]
  • sub_mesh_index (optional) — 0-based start indices of sub-meshes; if omitted, the AABB of the entire mesh is returned; [n_sub]
  • vec_size (optional) — Alignment size for SIMD/CUDA padding (e.g., 8 for AVX2, 32 for CUDA)
Returns:
  • arma::Mat<dtype> of shape [n_sub_aligned, 6], one AABB per sub-mesh row
See also:
  • ray_triangle_intersect (consumer of the output)

triangle_mesh_segmentation
Reorganize a 3D triangular mesh into spatially clustered sub-meshes for faster processing
  • Recursively partitions mesh by axis-aligned bounding box until each sub-mesh contains no more than target_size triangles
  • Output mesh retains all original triangles but in reordered sequence; sub-meshes are padded with zero-sized dummy triangles to align row counts to vec_size
  • Dummy triangles are placed at the AABB center of their sub-mesh; mesh_index uses 0 to mark padding entries
  • If mtl_ind is provided, material indices are reordered and padded the same way (padding uses index 0)
Declaration:
arma::uword triangle_mesh_segmentation(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *mesh,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *meshR,
    arma::u32_vec *sub_mesh_index,
    arma::uword target_size = 1024,
    arma::uword vec_size = 1,
    const arma::uvec *mtl_ind = nullptr,
    arma::uvec *mtl_ind_out = nullptr,
    arma::u32_vec *mesh_index = nullptr);
Inputs:
  • mesh — Triangle vertices, each row {x1,y1,z1,x2,y2,z2,x3,y3,z3}; [n_mesh, 9]
  • target_size (optional) — Target triangle count per sub-mesh; for best performance set near sqrt(n_mesh)
  • vec_size (optional) — SIMD/GPU alignment size (e.g. 8 for AVX2, 32 for CUDA); each sub-mesh row count rounded up to a multiple of this value
  • mtl_ind (optional) — Material indices per triangle (the csv_ind output of obj_file_read); [n_mesh]
Outputs:
  • meshR — Reordered and padded triangle vertices; [n_meshR, 9]
  • sub_mesh_index — 0-based start indices of sub-meshes in meshR; [n_sub]
  • mtl_ind_out (optional) — Reordered and padded material indices (padding = 0); [n_meshR]
  • mesh_index (optional) — 1-based row in mesh of each row of meshR (0 = padding); [n_meshR]
Returns:
  • Number of created sub-meshes n_sub
See also:
  • calc_diffraction_gain (uses sub_mesh_index for acceleration)
  • obj_file_read (defines mtl_ind / csv_ind)

triangle_mesh_split
Split a 3D triangular mesh into two sub-meshes along a given axis
  • Splits at the bounding box center of the selected axis; triangles where all vertices lie within the lower half go to meshA; any triangle with at least one vertex exceeding the threshold goes to meshB
  • axis = 0 selects the axis with the longest bounding box extent automatically
  • On failure (all triangles fall to one side), meshA and meshB are left unchanged and the return value is negative
  • Used internally by triangle_mesh_segmentation
Declaration:
int triangle_mesh_split(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *mesh,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *meshA,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *meshB,
    int axis = 0,
    arma::Col<int> *split_ind = nullptr);
Inputs:
  • mesh — Triangle vertices, each row {x1,y1,z1,x2,y2,z2,x3,y3,z3}; [n_mesh, 9]
  • axis (optional) — Split axis: 0 = longest extent, 1 = x, 2 = y, 3 = z
Outputs:
  • meshA — Triangles with all vertices within the lower half of the bounding box; [n_meshA, 9]
  • meshB — Triangles with at least one vertex exceeding the split threshold; [n_meshB, 9]
  • split_ind (optional) — Per-triangle assignment: 1 = meshA, 2 = meshB, 0 = unassigned (failure); [n_mesh]
Returns:
  • Axis used for the split (1, 2, or 3); negative (-1, -2, or -3) on failure
See also:
  • triangle_mesh_segmentation (calls this function recursively)

write_png
Write a data matrix to a color-coded PNG file
  • Values are clipped to [min_val, max_val] before colormap mapping; auto-detected from data if NAN
  • The auto-detected range ignores NaN and +-Inf; +Inf and -Inf values get the last and first colour
  • NaN values get the first colour of the map
  • A degenerate range (max <= min after auto-detection, e.g. constant or single-value data, all-NaN data, or log input without a positive value; also an infinite min_val or max_val) maps every value to the first colour
  • Throws std::invalid_argument for an empty data matrix and for min_val > max_val (both given)
  • Uses [LodePNG](https://github.com/lvandeve/lodepng) for PNG encoding
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::write_png(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &data,
    std::string fn,
    std::string colormap = "jet",
    dtype min_val = NAN,
    dtype max_val = NAN,
    bool log_transform = false);
Inputs:
  • data — Input data matrix
  • fn — Output .png file path
  • colormap (optional) — Colormap name; see colormap for valid values
  • min_val (optional) — Lower clipping bound; auto-detected if NAN
  • max_val (optional) — Upper clipping bound; auto-detected if NAN
  • log_transform (optional) — Apply 10*log10(data) before mapping; non-positive values map to the minimum color

xpr_update
Thread a polarization transfer (Jones) matrix along a ray path
  • Left-multiplies each per-ray state matrix by a new interaction matrix, vectorized across up to hundreds of millions of rays, then optionally normalizes the touched columns and re-imposes a separately tracked scalar gain — all in a single pass
  • One entry point covers every operation needed while threading a chain: initialization, updating, gain extraction, normalization, and gain application. They are selected independently through initialize, update, normalize, gain, and apply_gain, so a call can do any combination (see Usage)
  • An optional ray_index lets the update set be a subset of the global state, scattered into xprmat by column. Without it the mapping is 1:1 and n_ray == n_rayU
  • Storage is interleaved complex, column-major, matching the xprmat output of calc_diffraction_gain. EM mode carries the full 2x2 Jones matrix; scalar mode carries a single complex pressure coefficient
Declaration:
void xpr_update(
    arma::Mat<dtype> &xprmat,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *update = nullptr,
    arma::Col<dtype> *gain = nullptr,
    bool initialize = false,
    bool normalize = false,
    bool apply_gain = false,
    const arma::uvec *ray_index = nullptr);
Arguments:
  • xprmat — Global XPR state, updated in place. EM mode [8, n_ray], scalar mode [2, n_ray]. If empty on entry it is sized from update and seeded to unity (see Usage). A pre-sized (non-empty) buffer is treated as accumulated state unless initialize is set. When ray_index is given, xprmat must already exist, because the extent of the global set cannot be inferred from a subset.
  • update — Interaction matrix left-multiplied onto each ray of the update set. Layout must match xprmat (8 or 2 rows). Shapes: [8 or 2, n_rayU] applies one matrix per update; [8 or 2, 1] broadcasts one matrix to every update; nullptr or empty applies the identity (no multiply).
  • gain — Dual-purpose, length n_rayU; direction set by apply_gain:
    • apply_gain == false (output): resized to [n_rayU] and filled with each ray's gain before normalization. With normalize == false this measures the gain without modifying the matrix part of xprmat (gain extraction); with normalize == true it reports the gain that was removed. A value of 0 flags a degenerate (zero / non-finite) matrix the caller should prune.
    • apply_gain == true (input): read, not resized; each touched column is multiplied by the corresponding gain value after normalization. Must be non-null and length n_rayU.
  • initialize — If true, each touched column is set to unity (identity for EM, [1,0] for scalar) before the update. With an update this is a copy-through (the column becomes the update, prior contents discarded); without an update it is a pure reset. Use this to (re)initialize a pre-sized or reused buffer without reallocating.
  • normalize — If true, each touched column is scaled to unit gain after the update. Untouched columns of xprmat are left alone.
  • apply_gain — Flips gain from output to input (see above). Applying a gain requires a gain vector.
  • ray_index — Optional map from update index (0..n_rayU-1) to global column of xprmat. Length n_rayU. Default is the identity map, which requires n_ray == n_rayU. Values must be < n_ray. Duplicate values are allowed: the updates of a column are then applied one after the other in update-index order (each with the full initialize -> update -> normalize -> apply_gain sequence), so the result is the same as a single-threaded run for any thread count. With duplicates the call runs single-threaded; unique indices keep the parallel loop.
Usage:
The flags are composable in one call; the per-column order is always initialize -> update -> normalize -> apply_gain. Argument order is (xprmat, update, gain, initialize, normalize, apply_gain, ray_index). 
arma::Mat<dtype> M;                                 // ReVV ImVV ReHV ImHV ReVH ImVH ReHH ImHH (EM), labels <out><in>

// (1) Initialization
xpr_update(M, &U0);                                 // empty M: sized from U0, seeded, M := U0
xpr_update(M, nullptr, nullptr, true);              // pre-sized M: reset every column to unity
xpr_update(M, &U0, nullptr, true);                  // pre-sized M: copy-through, M := U0 (ignores old)

// (2) Updating (accumulate onto existing state)
xpr_update(M, &U);                                  // M := U * M
xpr_update(M, &Usub, nullptr, false, false, false, &idx);   // scatter: M[:,idx[k]] := Usub[:,k] * M[:,idx[k]]

// (3) Gain extraction (measure, leave the matrix part untouched)
arma::Col<dtype> g;
xpr_update(M, nullptr, &g);                         // g[k] = gain( M[:,k] )
xpr_update(M, &U, &g);                              // apply U, then measure into g (no normalize)

// (4) Normalizing (scale each column to unit gain; optionally update first)
xpr_update(M, nullptr, nullptr, false, true);       // M[:,k] := M[:,k] / gain( M[:,k] )
xpr_update(M, &U, nullptr, false, true);            // update, then normalize, one pass

// (5) Normalizing + gain application (strip the matrix's own magnitude, impose a tracked scalar).
//     'g' is read here (apply_gain = true):
xpr_update(M, nullptr, &g, false, true, true);      // M[:,k] := g[k] * ( M[:,k] / gain( M[:,k] ) )
xpr_update(M, &U, &g, false, true, true, &idx);     // full chain step: scatter+update+normalize+apply
Storage layout (column-major, interleaved complex):
  • EM mode, 8 values per ray: [ReVV ImVV ReHV ImHV ReVH ImVH ReHH ImHH], so column 0 of the 2x2 is [VV; HV] (response to a V input) and column 1 is [VH; HH] (response to an H input). The labels are <out><in>: the first letter is the output (receive) polarisation, the second the input (transmit) polarisation, so rows 0-1 = (V out, V in), rows 2-3 = (H out, V in), rows 4-5 = (V out, H in), rows 6-7 = (H out, H in). This is the row order of M in get_channels_spherical (rows = RX, columns = TX).
  • Scalar mode, 2 values per ray: [Re Im].
  • The update is M_out = M_update * M_state (new interaction left-multiplies), consistent with E_out = M * E_in for column vectors and Armadillo/MATLAB column-major storage.
See also:
  • calc_diffraction_gain (produces xprmat in this exact layout)


Sum-of-sinusoids random number generator

These functions generate spatially correlated random numbers: values that are random, but similar at nearby positions. Two positions that are much closer than the decorrelation distance get almost the same value, two positions that are far apart get independent values. The decorrelation distance is the distance at which the correlation has fallen to exp(-1), about 0.37. Typical uses are shadow fading maps and the spatially consistent parameters of the 3GPP TR 38.901 channel model.

The values are a sum of sinusoids, s(x) = sqrt(2/N) · sum_n cos( 2·pi · f_n·x + phi_n ). The frequencies f_n set how the correlation falls with the distance, i.e. the autocorrelation function (ACF). The random phases phi_n select one realisation of the random field. The generator needs no map and no grid: positions can be anywhere, in 1, 2 or 3 dimensions, and the same frequencies and phases always give the same field.

Quick start
sos_randn sets up the generators, draws the phases and returns the values in one call. sos_rand and sos_randi do the same for Uniform and integer values. 
#include "quadriga_lib.hpp"

// 1000 positions; one row per position, columns are {x, y, z} in meters
arma::mat pos(1000, 3, arma::fill::randu);
pos *= 100.0;

// One independent generator per decorrelation distance: 10 m and 50 m
arma::vec dist_decorr = {10.0, 50.0};

// Normal distributed values with zero mean and unit variance, size [1000, 2]
arma::mat val;
quadriga_lib::sos_randn<double>(dist_decorr, pos, val, nullptr, nullptr, "Comb256", 42);

// Uniform values between 0 and 1, and integers from 1 to 6
arma::mat uni, dice;
quadriga_lib::sos_rand<double>(dist_decorr, pos, uni, nullptr, nullptr, "Comb256", 42);
quadriga_lib::sos_randi<double>(dist_decorr, pos, 6, dice, nullptr, nullptr, "Comb256", 42);
The last argument is the seed. With the same seed, decorrelation distances and preset, a later call returns the same field, also at other positions. Without a seed every call gives a new field.

A seed selects a set of phases, not a field. Calls with the same seed but another decorrelation distance or another preset return related fields: they are equal at the coordinate origin and correlated around it. Quantities that must be independent of each other, such as shadow fading and delay spread, therefore need different seeds, or one call with one entry of dist_decorr per quantity: the generators of one call are independent.

Keeping a generator
A generator is two matrices: the frequencies from sos_preset and the phases from sos_init_phases. sos_val evaluates them. Each column of the phase matrix is one independent generator. Evaluating many generators in one call is much cheaper than one call per generator, because the sines and cosines of the positions are shared: 20 generators take less than twice as long as one. 
// Frequencies of a predefined generator for a decorrelation distance of 10 m
arma::mat freq;
arma::vec acf, dist;
quadriga_lib::sos_preset<double>("Comb256", freq, &acf, &dist, 10.0);

// Phases of 20 generators; each column is one independent random field
arma::mat phase;
quadriga_lib::sos_init_phases<double>(freq.n_rows, 20, phase, 42);

// Values of all 20 generators at the positions, size [1000, 20]
arma::mat val_all;
quadriga_lib::sos_val(freq, phase, pos, val_all);

// The same fields at other positions, at any later time
arma::mat pos_new = pos + 2.5, val_new;
quadriga_lib::sos_val(freq, phase, pos_new, val_new);

// Uniform distributed output
arma::mat val_uni;
quadriga_lib::sos_val<double>(freq, phase, pos, val_uni, nullptr, nullptr, nullptr, nullptr, 1.0, true);

// Map on a regular grid, one cube of size [n_y, n_x, 1] per generator
arma::vec x = arma::regspace(0.0, 0.5, 250.0), y = arma::regspace(0.0, 0.5, 100.0);
std::vector<arma::cube> grid;
quadriga_lib::sos_map<double>(freq, phase, x, grid, &y);
  • Presets: Exp, Gauss and Comb, each with 128, 256, 512 or 1024 sinusoids, e.g. "Exp512". Exp is the exponential ACF exp(-d/d0), Gauss is exp(-(d/d0)^2), and Comb follows Gauss up to the decorrelation distance d0 and Exp beyond it. Each doubling of the number of sinusoids doubles the computing time and lowers the error of the ACF by 3 to 4 dB: sos_calc_mse reports 28, 31, 34 and 38 dB for Exp128 to Exp1024 and 31, 35, 39 and 42 dB for Comb128 to Comb1024. "Comb256" is a good default; take a larger table if the shape of the ACF matters, a smaller one if speed does.
  • Both ends mobile: if a value belongs to a link between two moving devices, pass both positions. The value then changes smoothly when either device moves.
// Positions of the two ends of 1000 links; one value per link and generator, size [1000, 20]
arma::mat pos_tx = pos, pos_rx(1000, 3, arma::fill::randu), val_link;
pos_rx *= 100.0;
quadriga_lib::sos_val<double>(freq, phase, pos_tx, val_link, &pos_rx, nullptr, &acf, &dist);
  • Mean over an area: the values at positions that lie within a few decorrelation distances of each other are similar, so their average is not zero for a single realisation. This is how a correlated field behaves. sos_remove_bias can force the average to zero if an application needs it. This removes variance: for 200 positions in a square with an edge of 20 decorrelation distances 2.5 %, for an edge of 5 decorrelation distances 14 %, for 2 already 44 %. It is meant for positions that spread over many decorrelation distances.
  • Coordinates: use local coordinates. The argument f_n·x of a sinusoid is computed in double precision and is known to about 1e-16 of its size, so the values lose accuracy with the distance from the origin counted in wavelengths. Up to 1e9 wavelengths (more than 1e9 m for a decorrelation distance of 10 m) the results stay within the tolerance stated in sos_val. The float versions of the functions read positions in single precision, i.e. rounded to about 1 mm at a distance of 10 km.
  • Threads and memory: the functions that take noticeable time use the OpenMP threads of the process, except sos_generate, which runs on one thread. No result depends on the number of threads. sos_val computes in its output: for double it needs no further memory of that size for one device and one more array of that size for two devices; for float one array in double precision is added.
Own autocorrelation functions
sos_generate finds the frequencies for any ACF that is given as a table over the distance, and sos_calc_mse reports how well the result fits. The table should reach to about 5 decorrelation distances. 
// Target ACF from 0 to 5 decorrelation distances, here a Gaussian shape with 5 m
arma::vec dist_own = arma::regspace(0.0, 0.125, 24.875);
arma::vec acf_own = arma::exp(-arma::square(dist_own / 5.0));

// 256 sinusoids in 3-D, 300 test directions, wavelengths up to 20 decorrelation distances (100 m)
arma::mat freq_own;
quadriga_lib::sos_generate<double>(acf_own, dist_own, 256, freq_own, nullptr, nullptr, 3, true, 300, true,
                                   0.01, 0.0, 1);

// Approximation error in dB, larger is better; about 35 dB for this example
double mse = quadriga_lib::sos_calc_mse(freq_own, acf_own, dist_own);
The result is used like a preset: draw phases with sos_init_phases and evaluate with sos_val. To change the decorrelation distance afterwards, divide the frequencies and multiply the distances by the same factor.

Reference
S. Jaeckel, L. Raschkowski, F. Burkhardt, L. Thiele; "Efficient Sum-of-Sinusoids-Based Spatial Consistency for the 3GPP New-Radio Channel Model"; 2018 IEEE Globecom Workshops (GC Wkshps); 2018; Available: IEEE Xplore (Original version), PDF version, Markdown version


sos_acf_2d
Target ACF on a 2-D grid
  • Evaluates a rotationally symmetric autocorrelation function (ACF), given as a table over the distance, on a square grid
  • Grid axis: from -2·Dmax to 2·Dmax with the spacing of dist, where Dmax is the last sample point; n_grid = 4·n_acf - 3, the center is element 2·n_acf - 2 (0-based)
  • Linear interpolation of the table at the radial distance; 0 at radial distances above Dmax
  • The result is the reference for sos_acf_approx
  • Equally spaced means that every sample point lies within 1e-4 of the step from its place on the regular axis; for long single-precision tables the rounding of the sample points themselves is allowed for
  • dist_2d may be the same object as acf or dist
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_acf_2d(
    const arma::Col<dtype> &acf,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &dist,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &acf_2d,
    arma::Col<dtype> *dist_2d = nullptr);
Inputs:
  • acf — Target ACF; at least 2 elements; [n_acf]
  • dist — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, be equally spaced and finite; [n_acf]
Outputs:
  • acf_2d — ACF on the grid; [n_grid, n_grid]
  • dist_2d (optional) — Grid axis in [m]; [n_grid]
See also:
  • sos_acf_approx (ACF produced by a set of sinusoids on the same grid)
  • sos_calc_mse (difference between the two)

sos_acf_approx
ACF produced by a set of sinusoids
  • Autocorrelation function (ACF) of the values of sos_val over uniformly random phases: R(dx) = (1/n_coeff) · sum_n cos( 2·pi · f_n·dx )
  • Evaluated on the grid axis of sos_acf_2d: from -2·Dmax to 2·Dmax with the spacing of dist, n_grid = 4·n_acf - 3
  • n_dim = 1: along the axis; [n_grid, 1, 1]
  • n_dim = 2: on the x-y plane; [n_grid, n_grid, 1]; element (iy, ix)
  • n_dim = 3: on the x-y, x-z and y-z plane (slices 0, 1, 2); [n_grid, n_grid, 3]; the row index belongs to the second axis of the plane, the column index to the first
  • Only the sample points dist are used, not the ACF values
  • R(-dx) = R(dx): each plane is point-symmetric about its center. One half is computed and the other half is its mirror image, so the symmetry is exact
  • Equally spaced has the meaning given in sos_acf_2d
  • dist_2d may be the same object as dist
  • acf_approx cannot share memory with freq or dist; such a call throws before anything is written
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_acf_approx(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &dist,
    arma::Cube<dtype> &acf_approx,
    arma::Col<dtype> *dist_2d = nullptr);
Inputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; n_dim = 1, 2 or 3; [n_coeff, n_dim]
  • dist — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, be equally spaced and finite; at least 2 elements; [n_acf]
Outputs:
  • acf_approx — Approximated ACF; size depends on n_dim, see above
  • dist_2d (optional) — Grid axis in [m]; [n_grid]
See also:
  • sos_acf_2d (target ACF on the same grid)
  • sos_calc_mse (difference between the two)

sos_acf_estimate
Monte-Carlo estimate of the ACF of one generator
  • Estimates the autocorrelation function (ACF) of one realisation of a generator from its values at random positions, without using the frequencies other than through sos_val
  • Draws 2·n_pos positions, uniformly in a cube with half edge 0.4·max(bins) around the origin, evaluates the generator there, and for each distance bin computes the correlation coefficient between the two values of all position pairs whose distance falls into the bin
  • Positions: a matrix [2·n_pos, 3] is filled column by column (all x, then all y, then all z) with ( 2·u - 1 ) · 0.4·max(bins), where u comes from the random stream described in sos_init_phases
  • Bin n covers the distances from the midpoint between bins(n-1) and bins(n) (excluded) to the midpoint between bins(n) and bins(n+1) (included). The first bin starts at 1e-5 m, the last bin ends half a mean bin spacing above bins(end)
  • Bins with fewer than 6 pairs return NaN in all outputs
  • acf_est_dual: the same for two devices on the same map. The first n_pos positions are the first device, the second n_pos positions the second device, and the value of a link is sos_val with both positions. Two links form a pair if the distance between their first devices falls into the bin and the distance between their second devices does not exceed the upper limit of the bin; requires acf and dist
  • The estimate describes one realisation; it scatters around the ACF of the sinusoids
  • The pairs are searched in parallel and added in a fixed order; the result does not depend on the number of threads. Run time and memory: 2·n_pos^2 pairs for one device, at most 36 MB for the pairs of a block of rows
  • The outputs may be the same objects as inputs
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_acf_estimate(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &phase,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &bins,
    arma::uword n_pos,
    arma::Col<dtype> &acf_est,
    arma::Col<dtype> *dist_est = nullptr,
    arma::Col<dtype> *acf_est_dual = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *acf = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *dist = nullptr,
    arma::sword seed = -1);
Inputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; [n_coeff, 3]
  • phase — Phases of the generator in [rad]; [n_coeff]
  • bins — Bin centers in [m]; must be >= 0, increase strictly and be finite; at least 2 elements; [n_bin]
  • n_pos — Number of random positions per device; must be > 1
  • acf (optional) — Target ACF; required for acf_est_dual, must then be > -1; [n_acf]
  • dist (optional) — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, increase strictly and be finite; [n_acf]
  • seed (optional) — Seed for the positions; must be >= -1; -1 seeds from the system random device
Outputs:
  • acf_est — Estimated ACF; [n_bin]
  • dist_est (optional) — Mean distance of the pairs in each bin in [m]; [n_bin]
  • acf_est_dual (optional) — Estimated ACF for two devices on the same map; [n_bin]
See also:
  • sos_acf_approx (ACF of the sinusoids in closed form)
  • sos_val (evaluation of the generator)

sos_acfi
Target ACF at given distances
  • Linear interpolation of the autocorrelation function (ACF) given as a table
  • Distances beyond the last sample point return the last value of the table
  • The same rule is applied by sos_val and sos_remove_bias for two devices on the same map
  • An empty d returns an empty val
  • val may be the same object as acf, dist or d
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_acfi(
    const arma::Col<dtype> &acf,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &dist,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &d,
    arma::Col<dtype> &val);
Inputs:
  • acf — Target ACF; at least 2 elements; [n_acf]
  • dist — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, increase strictly and be finite; [n_acf]
  • d — Distances in [m]; must be >= 0; [n_d]
Output:
  • val — ACF at the distances d; [n_d]
See also:
  • sos_preset (ACF tables of the predefined models)

sos_calc_mse
Approximation error of the ACF of a set of sinusoids
  • Compares the autocorrelation function (ACF) produced by the sinusoids, R(dx) = (1/n_coeff) · sum_n cos( 2·pi · f_n·dx ), with the target ACF
  • All errors are returned as -10·log10( mean squared error ); larger is better
  • Return value: error along the test directions on the sample points dist. n_dim = 1: one direction; n_dim = 2: n_dir directions equally spaced on the half circle, azimuth pi · i / n_dir; n_dim = 3: n_dir directions on a spiral (Fibonacci) lattice on the upper hemisphere, sin(elevation) = ( i + 0.5 ) / n_dir, azimuth i · pi · ( 3 - sqrt(5) ). A direction and its opposite give the same ACF
  • mse_core: error on the grid of sos_acf_approx against sos_acf_2d, for the grid points within a radius of n_acf grid steps around the center; for n_dim = 3 the mean squared error is averaged over the three planes
  • mse_all: same on the complete grid, which extends to 2·Dmax and has a target of 0 beyond Dmax
  • For n_dim = 1, mse_core is evaluated on the sample points dist, mse_all on the axis from -2·Dmax to 2·Dmax, and the return value equals mse_core
  • acf_tail: approximated ACF, averaged over the test directions, at 2, 4, 10 and 20 times Dmax. The target is 0 there; sinusoids with a frequency near zero show up as a plateau of height n_low / n_coeff, which adds a constant offset to all values of a large area
  • mse_core and mse_all need the 2-D grids and are only computed when requested
  • A perfect fit has a mean squared error of 0 and is returned as +Inf dB; this happens for a target that the sinusoids reproduce exactly at all sample points, e.g. sinusoids at zero frequency and a target of 1
  • The test directions are evaluated in parallel; the result does not depend on the number of threads
  • Equally spaced has the meaning given in sos_acf_2d
  • acf_tail and test_dir may be the same objects as inputs
Declaration:
dtype quadriga_lib::sos_calc_mse(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &acf,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &dist,
    dtype *mse_core = nullptr,
    dtype *mse_all = nullptr,
    arma::Col<dtype> *acf_tail = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *test_dir = nullptr,
    arma::uword n_dir = 10);
Inputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; n_dim = 1, 2 or 3; [n_coeff, n_dim]
  • acf — Target ACF; at least 2 elements; [n_acf]
  • dist — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, be equally spaced and finite; [n_acf]
  • n_dir (optional) — Number of test directions; must be > 0; ignored for n_dim = 1
Outputs:
  • mse_core (optional) — Error within the core radius in [dB]
  • mse_all (optional) — Error on the complete grid in [dB]
  • acf_tail (optional) — Approximated ACF beyond the fitted range; [4]
  • test_dir (optional) — Test directions in [rad]; [n_dir, 2] with columns {azimuth, elevation} for n_dim = 3; [n_dir, 1] with the azimuth for n_dim = 2; empty for n_dim = 1
Returns:
  • Error along the test directions in [dB]
See also:
  • sos_generate (finds the frequencies; minimises the error returned here)
  • sos_acf_approx, sos_acf_2d (the two grids that are compared)

sos_generate
Find the frequencies of a sum-of-sinusoids generator for a given ACF
  • Searches n_coeff frequencies such that the autocorrelation function (ACF) of the sinusoids, R(dx) = (1/n_coeff) · sum_n cos( 2·pi · f_n·dx ), approximates the target ACF in all directions
  • Each sinusoid has a fixed direction and a signed frequency along it. n_dim = 1: no direction; n_dim = 2: directions on the half circle; n_dim = 3: directions on the upper hemisphere. uniform_smp = true places them regularly (equally spaced azimuth, spiral lattice, as the test directions of sos_calc_mse), false draws them at random
  • Iteration: in each pass every sinusoid is re-fitted once. Its frequency is chosen to fit the residual between the target and the contribution of all other sinusoids along its own direction: coarse search over a grid with a spacing of 0.1 / max(dist), then a step refinement. The new frequency is kept only if the mean squared error over the test directions decreases. The iteration stops after a pass that changes nothing or lowers the error by less than a factor of 1e-6, or after 1000 passes
  • random_init = true starts from uniformly random frequencies; false builds the set up sinusoid by sinusoid in the first pass
  • The fit covers the range of dist only. The ACF of the result beyond the last sample point is not controlled; sinusoids with a frequency near zero keep it above zero at all larger distances (see acf_tail of sos_calc_mse). To prevent this, set freq_min; the tables of sos_preset use 0.05 / d with the decorrelation distance d
  • Use enough test directions: with few (e.g. 10) the result fits those directions and is worse in others; the tables of sos_preset were made with 300
  • freq_min and freq_max limit the magnitude of the frequency along the direction of each sinusoid; 0 selects the default: no lower limit, and 15 / max(dist) as upper limit
  • The search grid has ( freq_max - freq_min ) · max(dist) / 0.1 + 1 points, and its cosines are stored for all sample points. It must not have more than 100000 points (the default has 151), which limits freq_max - freq_min to less than 10000 / max(dist)
  • The function runs on one thread
  • ase and test_dir may be the same objects as acf or dist
  • test_dir and the reported error use the same test directions as sos_calc_mse with the same n_dir
  • All computations are done in double precision for both instantiations
  • A seed >= 0 makes the result repeatable on one platform. The search takes accept/reject decisions, so a difference in the last bit of the input or of the math library can lead to another table of equivalent quality
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_generate(
    const arma::Col<dtype> &acf,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &dist,
    arma::uword n_coeff,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    arma::Col<dtype> *ase = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *test_dir = nullptr,
    arma::uword n_dim = 3,
    bool uniform_smp = true,
    arma::uword n_dir = 10,
    bool random_init = true,
    dtype freq_min = 0.0,
    dtype freq_max = 0.0,
    arma::sword seed = -1);
Inputs:
  • acf — Target ACF; at least 2 elements; [n_acf]
  • dist — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, increase strictly and be finite; [n_acf]
  • n_coeff — Number of sinusoids; must be > 0
  • n_dim (optional) — Number of dimensions; 1, 2 or 3
  • uniform_smp (optional) — Regular (true) or random (false) directions of the sinusoids
  • n_dir (optional) — Number of test directions; must be > 0; ignored for n_dim = 1
  • random_init (optional) — Start from random frequencies (true) or build up sinusoid by sinusoid (false)
  • freq_min (optional) — Lower limit of the frequency magnitude in [1/m]; must be >= 0; 0 = none
  • freq_max (optional) — Upper limit of the frequency magnitude in [1/m]; must be >= 0 and, if not 0, larger than freq_min; 0 = 15 / max(dist); limited by the size of the search grid, see above
  • seed (optional) — Seed; must be >= -1; -1 seeds from the system random device
Outputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; [n_coeff, n_dim]
  • ase (optional) — Mean squared error over the test directions after each pass, linear scale; [n_pass]
  • test_dir (optional) — Test directions in [rad]; [n_dir, 2] with columns {azimuth, elevation} for n_dim = 3; [n_dir, 1] with the azimuth for n_dim = 2; empty for n_dim = 1
See also:
  • sos_calc_mse (error of a frequency table)
  • sos_preset (precomputed tables)

sos_init_phases
Draw random phases for a set of sum-of-sinusoids generators
  • Each column of phase is one realisation of the random field of a generator; see sos_val
  • Values are uniform in the interval from -pi (included) to pi (excluded)
  • Random stream: std::mt19937_64 seeded with seed; each value is 2·pi · ( u - 0.5 ), where u is the upper 53 bits of one generator output divided by 2^53; no distribution class of the standard library is used, so a seed >= 0 gives the same phases on all platforms
  • The matrix is filled column by column from one stream: for the same seed, column g does not depend on n_gen
  • For dtype = float the values are rounded from double; a value that rounds to pi is returned as -pi
  • A seed stands for a set of phases. Generators that use phases of the same seed with different frequencies (another decorrelation distance or preset) produce related fields, which are equal at the coordinate origin and correlated around it; draw independent quantities as columns of one call or with different seeds
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_init_phases(
    arma::uword n_coeff,
    arma::uword n_gen,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &phase,
    arma::sword seed = -1);
Inputs:
  • n_coeff — Number of sinusoids; must be > 0
  • n_gen — Number of generators; must be > 0
  • seed (optional) — Seed of the random stream; must be >= -1; -1 seeds from the system random device and gives different phases on each call
Output:
  • phase — Phases in [rad]; [n_coeff, n_gen]
See also:
  • sos_val (evaluates the generators)
  • sos_preset (frequencies that belong to the phases)

sos_map
Spatially correlated random values on a regular grid
  • Evaluates the generators of sos_val for one device at all combinations of the grid vectors
  • Output in meshgrid order: element (iy, ix, iz) of a cube belongs to the position {x(ix), y(iy), z(iz)}
  • One cube per generator, i.e. per column of phase
  • The number of grid vectors given must not exceed the number of columns of freq; an omitted or empty y or z means a single sample at 0 on that axis
  • z can be given without y if freq has 3 columns
  • The values are those of sos_val at the grid points, bit for bit; kernels, accuracy and the Uniform mapping are described there
  • Cubes of the correct size keep their memory. If they lie one after the other in memory (one block of n_gen cubes, as the MATLAB and Python interfaces allocate it), the values are computed in place; otherwise they are computed in a matrix [ny·nx·nz, n_gen] and copied
  • A cube that shares memory with freq or phase is an error, before anything is written
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_map(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &phase,
    const arma::Col<dtype> &x,
    std::vector<arma::Cube<dtype>> &map,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *y = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *z = nullptr,
    bool uniform = false,
    int use_kernel = 0);
Inputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; n_dim = 1, 2 or 3; [n_coeff, n_dim]
  • phase — Phases in [rad]; one column per generator; [n_coeff, n_gen]
  • x — Grid points on the x-axis in [m]; [nx]
  • y (optional) — Grid points on the y-axis in [m]; requires n_dim >= 2; omitted = one sample at 0, ny = 1; [ny]
  • z (optional) — Grid points on the z-axis in [m]; requires n_dim = 3; omitted = one sample at 0, nz = 1; [nz]
  • uniform (optional) — Output distribution: false = Normal(0,1), true = Uniform between 0 and 1
  • use_kernel (optional) — Compute kernel: 0 = auto (AVX2 if available), 1 = GENERIC, 2 = AVX2; throws if the requested kernel is unavailable
Output:
  • map — Values; vector of length n_gen with one cube of size [ny, nx, nz] per generator
See also:
  • sos_val (evaluation at arbitrary positions, two devices)

sos_preset
Frequencies and ACF of a predefined sum-of-sinusoids generator
  • Returns a precomputed frequency table for sos_val together with the autocorrelation function (ACF) it was fitted to
  • Presets: Exp128, Exp256, Exp512, Exp1024, Gauss128, Gauss256, Gauss512, Gauss1024, Comb128, Comb256, Comb512, Comb1024. The number is the number of sinusoids, n_coeff. The name is matched exactly, including upper and lower case
  • ACF types, with the distance D and the decorrelation distance d: Exp: R = exp( -D/d ); Gauss: R = exp( -(D/d)^2 ); Comb: Gauss for D <= d, Exp beyond. All three have R(d) = exp(-1)
  • The ACF is returned as a table with 200 samples from 0 to 4.975·d in steps of 0.025·d
  • The tables are stored for d = 10 m. For another decorrelation distance the distances are multiplied by d/10 and the frequencies by 10/d
  • All tables are 3-dimensional and can be used with positions of 1, 2 or 3 columns
  • The outputs cannot be the same object or share memory with each other; such a call throws before anything is written
  • How the tables were made: sos_generate with the 200-sample ACF for 10 m, n_dim = 3, uniform_smp = true, n_dir = 300, random_init = true, freq_min = 0.005, freq_max = 15 / 49.75, and the seeds 39, 3, 2, 5 (Exp), 13, 32, 6, 21 (Gauss), 13, 25, 37, 47 (Comb) for 128, 256, 512 and 1024 sinusoids. Sinusoid n points in direction n of the spiral lattice described in sos_calc_mse
  • The lower frequency limit means that no sinusoid has a wavelength above 20 decorrelation distances. The ACF of the tables therefore stays near zero beyond the fitted range (magnitude below 0.015 at 10, 20, 50 and 100 decorrelation distances), and the mean of the generator over a large area is not offset by sinusoids of nearly zero frequency
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_preset(
    const std::string &name,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    arma::Col<dtype> *acf = nullptr,
    arma::Col<dtype> *dist = nullptr,
    dtype dist_decorr = 10.0);
Inputs:
  • name — Name of the preset, e.g. "Comb256"
  • dist_decorr (optional) — Decorrelation distance in [m]; must be > 0
Outputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; [n_coeff, 3]
  • acf (optional) — Target ACF; [200]
  • dist (optional) — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; [200]
See also:
  • sos_init_phases (random phases for the generator)
  • sos_val (evaluation)
  • sos_generate (frequencies for other ACFs)

sos_rand
Spatially correlated Uniform distributed random numbers
  • Convenience function: sets up one sum-of-sinusoids generator per entry of dist_decorr from a preset, draws its phases and evaluates it at the given positions; see sos_val for the model
  • The generators are independent of each other and may have different decorrelation distances
  • Phases: generator g uses column g of sos_init_phases( n_coeff, n_gen, seed ). A second device on its own map uses column g of a second stream, sos_init_phases( n_coeff, n_gen, seed_b ), where seed_b is the seed with bit 62 flipped, i.e. seed + 2^62 for seeds below 2^62; for seed = -1 both streams are random. The phases of a generator therefore depend neither on the number of generators nor on whether a second device is given
  • A decorrelation distance of 0 is replaced by 0.1 m
  • One device (pos_b omitted): values of the generator at pos
  • Two devices, same decorrelation distance (dist_decorr_b omitted, or equal to dist_decorr for that generator): both devices are on the same map, the value does not change when they swap positions
  • Two devices, different decorrelation distances: each device has its own map; the value is the sum of both maps divided by sqrt(2)
  • The Normal distributed value s of the generator is mapped to 0.5 · erfc( -s / sqrt(2) )
  • Each call with seed = -1 gives a new realisation. With a seed >= 0 the phases are the same on all platforms: the same positions give the same values and nearby positions similar values, also in separate calls. The kernel is chosen automatically, so values from machines with and without AVX2 differ by the tolerance stated in sos_val
  • A seed selects the phases, not the field: calls with the same seed but other decorrelation distances or another preset return related fields, equal at the coordinate origin and correlated around it. Use different seeds, or several entries of dist_decorr in one call, for quantities that must be independent
  • val may be the same object as pos or pos_b
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_rand(
    const arma::Col<dtype> &dist_decorr,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &pos,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &val,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *pos_b = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *dist_decorr_b = nullptr,
    const std::string &preset = "Comb256",
    arma::sword seed = -1);
Inputs:
  • dist_decorr — Decorrelation distances in [m]; one generator per entry; must be >= 0; [n_gen]
  • pos — Positions of the first device in [m]; one row per position; the number of columns sets the number of dimensions: [n_pos, 1] = {x}, [n_pos, 2] = {x, y} or [n_pos, 3] = {x, y, z}; coordinates that are not given are 0
  • pos_b (optional) — Positions of the second device in [m]; omitted or empty = one device; [n_pos, 1], [n_pos, 2] or [n_pos, 3] as for pos; the number of columns may differ from pos
  • dist_decorr_b (optional) — Decorrelation distances of the second device in [m]; requires pos_b; must be >= 0; omitted or empty = same as dist_decorr; [n_gen]
  • preset (optional) — Name of the preset, see sos_preset
  • seed (optional) — Seed of the phases; must be >= -1; -1 seeds from the system random device
Output:
  • val — Values, approximately Uniform distributed between 0 and 1; one row per position, one column per generator; [n_pos, n_gen]
See also:
  • sos_val (evaluation with given frequencies and phases)
  • sos_preset, sos_init_phases (the two steps this function combines)
  • sos_randn, sos_randi (Normal and integer values)

sos_randi
Spatially correlated integer random numbers
  • Convenience function: sets up one sum-of-sinusoids generator per entry of dist_decorr from a preset, draws its phases and evaluates it at the given positions; see sos_val for the model
  • The generators are independent of each other and may have different decorrelation distances
  • Phases: generator g uses column g of sos_init_phases( n_coeff, n_gen, seed ). A second device on its own map uses column g of a second stream, sos_init_phases( n_coeff, n_gen, seed_b ), where seed_b is the seed with bit 62 flipped, i.e. seed + 2^62 for seeds below 2^62; for seed = -1 both streams are random. The phases of a generator therefore depend neither on the number of generators nor on whether a second device is given
  • A decorrelation distance of 0 is replaced by 0.1 m
  • One device (pos_b omitted): values of the generator at pos
  • Two devices, same decorrelation distance (dist_decorr_b omitted, or equal to dist_decorr for that generator): both devices are on the same map, the value does not change when they swap positions
  • Two devices, different decorrelation distances: each device has its own map; the value is the sum of both maps divided by sqrt(2)
  • The Uniform value u of sos_rand is mapped to ceil( u · imax ), limited to 1 ... imax
  • Each call with seed = -1 gives a new realisation. With a seed >= 0 the phases are the same on all platforms: the same positions give the same values and nearby positions similar values, also in separate calls. The kernel is chosen automatically, so values from machines with and without AVX2 differ by the tolerance stated in sos_val, and an integer can then differ by 1 where the Uniform value lies at a step
  • A seed selects the phases, not the field: calls with the same seed but other decorrelation distances or another preset return related fields, equal at the coordinate origin and correlated around it. Use different seeds, or several entries of dist_decorr in one call, for quantities that must be independent
  • val may be the same object as pos or pos_b
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_randi(
    const arma::Col<dtype> &dist_decorr,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &pos,
    arma::uword imax,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &val,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *pos_b = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *dist_decorr_b = nullptr,
    const std::string &preset = "Comb256",
    arma::sword seed = -1);
Inputs:
  • dist_decorr — Decorrelation distances in [m]; one generator per entry; must be >= 0; [n_gen]
  • pos — Positions of the first device in [m]; one row per position; the number of columns sets the number of dimensions: [n_pos, 1] = {x}, [n_pos, 2] = {x, y} or [n_pos, 3] = {x, y, z}; coordinates that are not given are 0
  • imax — Largest value; must be > 0
  • pos_b (optional) — Positions of the second device in [m]; omitted or empty = one device; [n_pos, 1], [n_pos, 2] or [n_pos, 3] as for pos; the number of columns may differ from pos
  • dist_decorr_b (optional) — Decorrelation distances of the second device in [m]; requires pos_b; must be >= 0; omitted or empty = same as dist_decorr; [n_gen]
  • preset (optional) — Name of the preset, see sos_preset
  • seed (optional) — Seed of the phases; must be >= -1; -1 seeds from the system random device
Output:
  • val — Integer values from 1 to imax, each with approximately the same probability; one row per position, one column per generator; [n_pos, n_gen]
See also:
  • sos_val (evaluation with given frequencies and phases)
  • sos_preset, sos_init_phases (the two steps this function combines)
  • sos_randn, sos_rand (Normal and Uniform values)

sos_randn
Spatially correlated Normal distributed random numbers
  • Convenience function: sets up one sum-of-sinusoids generator per entry of dist_decorr from a preset, draws its phases and evaluates it at the given positions; see sos_val for the model
  • The generators are independent of each other and may have different decorrelation distances
  • Phases: generator g uses column g of sos_init_phases( n_coeff, n_gen, seed ). A second device on its own map uses column g of a second stream, sos_init_phases( n_coeff, n_gen, seed_b ), where seed_b is the seed with bit 62 flipped, i.e. seed + 2^62 for seeds below 2^62; for seed = -1 both streams are random. The phases of a generator therefore depend neither on the number of generators nor on whether a second device is given
  • A decorrelation distance of 0 is replaced by 0.1 m
  • One device (pos_b omitted): values of the generator at pos
  • Two devices, same decorrelation distance (dist_decorr_b omitted, or equal to dist_decorr for that generator): both devices are on the same map, the value does not change when they swap positions
  • Two devices, different decorrelation distances: each device has its own map; the value is the sum of both maps divided by sqrt(2)
  • Each call with seed = -1 gives a new realisation. With a seed >= 0 the phases are the same on all platforms: the same positions give the same values and nearby positions similar values, also in separate calls. The kernel is chosen automatically, so values from machines with and without AVX2 differ by the tolerance stated in sos_val
  • A seed selects the phases, not the field: calls with the same seed but other decorrelation distances or another preset return related fields, equal at the coordinate origin and correlated around it. Use different seeds, or several entries of dist_decorr in one call, for quantities that must be independent
  • val may be the same object as pos or pos_b

    const arma::Col<dtype> *dist_decorr_b = nullptr,
    const std::string &preset = "Comb256",
    arma::sword seed = -1);
Inputs:
  • dist_decorr — Decorrelation distances in [m]; one generator per entry; must be >= 0; [n_gen]
  • pos — Positions of the first device in [m]; one row per position; the number of columns sets the number of dimensions: [n_pos, 1] = {x}, [n_pos, 2] = {x, y} or [n_pos, 3] = {x, y, z}; coordinates that are not given are 0
  • pos_b (optional) — Positions of the second device in [m]; omitted or empty = one device; [n_pos, 1], [n_pos, 2] or [n_pos, 3] as for pos; the number of columns may differ from pos
