sos_remove_bias
Remove the sample mean of sum-of-sinusoids generators over a set of positions
  • One realisation of a spatially correlated random field has a non-zero mean over a finite set of positions. Its variance over the realisations is the mean of the autocorrelation function (ACF) over all pairs of positions, which is much larger than 1 / n_pos when the positions lie within a few decorrelation distances of each other. This is a property of any correlated field, not an error of the generator
  • This function adds one offset to all phases of a generator such that the mean of the Normal distributed values of sos_val over the given positions becomes zero
  • Closed form, no iteration: with z_n = mean over the positions of exp( j·2·pi · f_n·x ) and Z = sum_n z_n · exp( j·phi_n ), the mean is sqrt(2/n_coeff) · Re( exp( j·offset ) · Z ), which vanishes for offset = pi/2 - arg(Z) and for that value minus pi; the one with the smaller magnitude is applied, so the offset lies between -pi/2 and pi/2
  • One device (pos_b omitted): one offset per generator
  • Two devices on the same map (pos_b given, phase_b omitted): one offset per generator that zeroes the mean of the combined, normalised value; acf and dist are required
  • Two devices on different maps (pos_b and phase_b given): each device is treated on its own, phase with pos and phase_b with pos_b and the frequencies freq · freq_scale_b; two offsets per generator. The mean of the combined value is then zero as well. phase_b cannot share memory with phase: one matrix cannot hold two corrections
  • The arguments that are written, phase, phase_b, offset and mean_before, cannot share memory with another argument, not even in part, and offset and mean_before cannot be the same object. Such a call throws and leaves all arguments unchanged
  • Effect on the statistics: the result is the field conditioned on a zero mean over the positions. The variance at a position drops from 1 to 1 - c^2 / v, where v is the variance of the sample mean and c the mean ACF between that position and all positions; on average over the positions the loss is at least v. It is small only if the positions spread over many decorrelation distances. For positions within about one decorrelation distance most of the variance is removed
  • Rule of thumb, measured for 200 positions in a square with the edge L and the decorrelation distance d (Comb256): the variance drops by 74 % for L = d, 44 % for L = 2·d, 14 % for L = 5·d, 5 % for L = 10·d, 2.5 % for L = 20·d and less than 1 % from L = 50·d on
  • The zero mean holds for exactly these positions and frequencies. It is lost when the phases are used with other frequencies (another decorrelation distance) or positions
  • The mean of the Uniform output of sos_val is not exactly 0.5 afterwards
  • Phases are returned in the interval from -pi (included) to pi (excluded). A generator whose abs(Z) is below 1e-14 · n_coeff already has a zero mean for every offset and is left unchanged, also if its phases lie outside that interval
  • Two devices on the same map need an ACF above -1 at all sample points, as in sos_val
  • The sums over the positions are computed in parallel over the sinusoids; the result does not depend on the number of threads
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_remove_bias(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &phase,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &pos,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *pos_b = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *phase_b = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *acf = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *dist = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *offset = nullptr,
    arma::Mat<dtype> *mean_before = nullptr,
    dtype freq_scale_b = 1.0);
Inputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; n_dim = 1, 2 or 3; [n_coeff, n_dim]
  • phase — Phases in [rad]; one column per generator; updated in-place; [n_coeff, n_gen]
  • pos — Positions of the first device in [m]; one row per position; the number of columns sets the number of dimensions: [n_pos, 1] = {x}, [n_pos, 2] = {x, y} or [n_pos, 3] = {x, y, z}; coordinates that are not given are 0; not more columns than freq
  • pos_b (optional) — Positions of the second device in [m]; omitted or empty = one device; [n_pos_b, 1], [n_pos_b, 2] or [n_pos_b, 3] as for pos; n_pos_b = n_pos for the same map, any number for different maps
  • phase_b (optional) — Phases of the second device in [rad]; requires pos_b; omitted or empty = same map; updated in-place; no memory shared with phase; [n_coeff, n_gen]
  • acf (optional) — Target ACF; required for two devices on the same map, must then be > -1; [n_acf]
  • dist (optional) — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, increase strictly and be finite; [n_acf]
  • freq_scale_b (optional) — Frequency scale of the second device, i.e. decorrelation distance of the first device divided by that of the second; must be > 0; other than 1 requires phase_b
Outputs:
  • offset (optional) — Offsets added to the phases in [rad]; [n_gen, 1], or [n_gen, 2] with phase_b (column 0: phase, column 1: phase_b)
  • mean_before (optional) — Sample mean before the correction; [n_gen, 1]: mean of the values of sos_val for the given arguments; with phase_b [n_gen, 2]: mean of the field of each device on its own positions, the mean of the combined value being their sum divided by sqrt(2)
See also:
  • sos_val (the values whose mean is removed)

sos_val
Spatially correlated random values at given positions
  • Sum-of-sinusoids generator: at position x a generator with n_coeff sinusoids produces s(x) = sqrt(2/n_coeff) · sum_n cos( 2·pi · f_n·x + phi_n ), with the frequencies f_n (rows of freq) and the phases phi_n (one column of phase)
  • For uniformly random phases, s has zero mean, unit variance and the autocorrelation function (ACF) (1/n_coeff) · sum_n cos( 2·pi · f_n·dx ), which approximates the target ACF the frequencies were fitted to; with many sinusoids the values are approximately Normal distributed
  • All generators of one call share the frequencies; each column of phase is one generator. The sines and cosines of the positions are computed once and reused for all generators
  • Positions may have fewer columns than freq; missing coordinates are 0
  • One device (pos_b omitted): val = s(pos)
  • Two devices on the same map (pos_b given, phase_b omitted): both devices use phase; val = ( s(pos) + s(pos_b) ) / sqrt( 2 + 2·A(D) ), where D is the distance between pos and pos_b and A the target ACF interpolated as in sos_acfi; acf and dist are required. The value does not change when the two devices swap their positions
  • Two devices on different maps (pos_b and phase_b given): val = ( s_a(pos) + s_b(pos_b) ) / sqrt(2); s_b uses phase_b and the frequencies freq · freq_scale_b; acf and dist are ignored
  • The mode follows from the arguments only; phase values are never compared. Passing the same matrix as phase and phase_b selects the two-map formula
  • freq_scale_b other than 1 requires phase_b: one map with two decorrelation distances has no consistent ACF
  • Two devices on the same map need an ACF above -1 at all sample points, because the value is divided by sqrt( 2 + 2·A(D) )
  • uniform = true maps the values to 0.5 · erfc( -val / sqrt(2) ), which is uniform between 0 and 1 as far as val is Normal distributed. The limits 0 and 1 are included: a value far out in the tail is rounded to them, for dtype = float every value above 5.4 gives 1
  • Kernels: GENERIC computes in double precision. AVX2 reduces the argument f_n·x to one period in double precision, evaluates sine and cosine in single precision and accumulates in double precision; its values differ from GENERIC by less than 4e-7 · sqrt( 2·n_coeff ), typically by less than 1e-6
  • Accuracy far from the origin: the argument f_n·x is known to about 1e-16 of its size. The bound between the kernels holds up to about 1e9 periods of the sinusoids; beyond that both kernels lose accuracy at the same rate, and from 2^53 periods on every position returns the value at the origin
  • A NaN or infinite coordinate gives NaN at that position and does not affect other positions
  • The value at a position does not depend on the other positions or generators of the call, nor on the number of threads
  • For dtype = float the inputs are converted to double, the computation is the same and the result is rounded to single precision
  • Memory: for dtype = double the values are computed in val; two devices need one more array of the size of val, and dtype = float one array of that size in double precision
  • val cannot be the same object as an input, nor use the memory of one: it is resized and written while the inputs are read. Such a call throws and leaves val unchanged
Declaration:
void quadriga_lib::sos_val(
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &freq,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &phase,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> &pos,
    arma::Mat<dtype> &val,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *pos_b = nullptr,
    const arma::Mat<dtype> *phase_b = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *acf = nullptr,
    const arma::Col<dtype> *dist = nullptr,
    dtype freq_scale_b = 1.0,
    bool uniform = false,
    int use_kernel = 0);
Inputs:
  • freq — Frequencies in [1/m]; n_dim = 1, 2 or 3; [n_coeff, n_dim]
  • phase — Phases in [rad]; one column per generator; [n_coeff, n_gen]
  • pos — Positions of the first device in [m]; one row per position; the number of columns sets the number of dimensions: [n_pos, 1] = {x}, [n_pos, 2] = {x, y} or [n_pos, 3] = {x, y, z}; coordinates that are not given are 0; not more columns than freq
  • pos_b (optional) — Positions of the second device in [m]; omitted or empty = one device; [n_pos, 1], [n_pos, 2] or [n_pos, 3] as for pos; the number of columns may differ from pos
  • phase_b (optional) — Phases of the second device in [rad]; requires pos_b; omitted or empty = same map; [n_coeff, n_gen]
  • acf (optional) — Target ACF; required for two devices on the same map, must then be > -1; [n_acf]
  • dist (optional) — Sample points of the ACF in [m]; must start at 0, increase strictly and be finite; [n_acf]
  • freq_scale_b (optional) — Frequency scale of the second device, i.e. decorrelation distance of the first device divided by that of the second; must be > 0
  • uniform (optional) — Output distribution: false = Normal(0,1), true = Uniform between 0 and 1
  • use_kernel (optional) — Compute kernel: 0 = auto (AVX2 if available), 1 = GENERIC, 2 = AVX2; throws if the requested kernel is unavailable
Output:
  • val — Values; one row per position, one column per generator; [n_pos, n_gen]
See also:
  • sos_preset (frequencies and ACF of the predefined models)
  • sos_init_phases (random phases)
  • sos_map (evaluation on a grid)
  • sos_randn (generator set-up and evaluation in one call)
